9.1 Searching Algorithms

9.1.1 Linear Search

earth
// Linear Search
define linear_search:
    // Basic linear search
    define search(arr, target):
        for i, element in enumerate(arr):
            if element == target:
                return i;
        return -1;
    
    // Linear search with early exit
    define search_sorted(arr, target):
        for i, element in enumerate(arr):
            if element == target:
                return i;
            if element > target:
                break;
        return -1;
    
    // Linear search with custom comparison
    define search_custom(arr, target, compare):
        for i, element in enumerate(arr):
            if compare(element, target) == 0:
                return i;
        return -1;
    
    // Linear search in 2D array
    define search_2d(matrix, target):
        for i in range(len(matrix)):
            for j in range(len(matrix[i])):
                if matrix[i][j] == target:
                    return (i, j);
        return (-1, -1);
    
    // Linear search for all occurrences
    define search_all(arr, target):
        positions = [];
        for i, element in enumerate(arr):
            if element == target:
                positions.append(i);
        return positions;
    
    // Linear search with sentinel
    define search_sentinel(arr, target):
        # Add sentinel at end
        arr_with_sentinel = arr + [target];
        i = 0;
        while arr_with_sentinel[i] != target:
            i += 1;
        if i < len(arr):
            return i;
        return -1;

// Linear Search Analysis
define linear_search_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = list(range(1000000));
    target = random.randint(0, 1000000);
    
    // Best case
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, arr[0]);  # First element
    best_time = time.time() - start;
    
    // Worst case
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, 1000001);  # Not found
    worst_time = time.time() - start;
    
    // Average case
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, target);
    avg_time = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Linear Search Performance:");
    print(f"Best case: {best_time:.6f} seconds");
    print(f"Average case: {avg_time:.6f} seconds");
    print(f"Worst case: {worst_time:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(n)
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Linear search analysis complete";
9.1.2 Binary Search

earth
// Binary Search (Iterative)
define binary_search:
    // Iterative binary search
    define search(arr, target):
        left = 0;
        right = len(arr) - 1;
        
        while left <= right:
            mid = (left + right) // 2;
            if arr[mid] == target:
                return mid;
            elif arr[mid] < target:
                left = mid + 1;
            else:
                right = mid - 1;
        return -1;
    
    // Recursive binary search
    define search_recursive(arr, target, left=0, right=null):
        if right is null:
            right = len(arr) - 1;
        if left > right:
            return -1;
        
        mid = (left + right) // 2;
        if arr[mid] == target:
            return mid;
        elif arr[mid] < target:
            return search_recursive(arr, target, mid + 1, right);
        else:
            return search_recursive(arr, target, left, mid - 1);
    
    // Binary search for first occurrence
    define search_first(arr, target):
        left = 0;
        right = len(arr) - 1;
        result = -1;
        
        while left <= right:
            mid = (left + right) // 2;
            if arr[mid] == target:
                result = mid;
                right = mid - 1;  # Search left for earlier occurrence
            elif arr[mid] < target:
                left = mid + 1;
            else:
                right = mid - 1;
        return result;
    
    // Binary search for last occurrence
    define search_last(arr, target):
        left = 0;
        right = len(arr) - 1;
        result = -1;
        
        while left <= right:
            mid = (left + right) // 2;
            if arr[mid] == target:
                result = mid;
                left = mid + 1;  # Search right for later occurrence
            elif arr[mid] < target:
                left = mid + 1;
            else:
                right = mid - 1;
        return result;
    
    // Binary search for insertion position
    define search_insert_position(arr, target):
        left = 0;
        right = len(arr);
        
        while left < right:
            mid = (left + right) // 2;
            if arr[mid] < target:
                left = mid + 1;
            else:
                right = mid;
        return left;

// Binary Search Analysis
define binary_search_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = list(range(1000000));
    target = random.randint(0, 1000000);
    
    // Best case
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, arr[500000]);  # Middle element
    best_time = time.time() - start;
    
    // Worst case
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, 1000001);  # Not found
    worst_time = time.time() - start;
    
    // Average case
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, target);
    avg_time = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Binary Search Performance:");
    print(f"Best case: {best_time:.6f} seconds");
    print(f"Average case: {avg_time:.6f} seconds");
    print(f"Worst case: {worst_time:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(log n)
    // Space complexity: O(1) iterative, O(log n) recursive
    
    return "Binary search analysis complete";
9.1.3 Jump Search

earth
// Jump Search
define jump_search:
    define search(arr, target):
        n = len(arr);
        if n == 0:
            return -1;
        
        # Calculate jump size
        step = int(n ** 0.5);
        
        # Find block containing target
        prev = 0;
        while prev < n and arr[min(step, n) - 1] < target:
            prev = step;
            step += int(n ** 0.5);
            if prev >= n:
                return -1;
        
        # Linear search in block
        for i in range(prev, min(step, n)):
            if arr[i] == target:
                return i;
        return -1;
    
    // Jump search with custom step size
    define search_custom_step(arr, target, step):
        n = len(arr);
        prev = 0;
        while prev < n and arr[min(step, n) - 1] < target:
            prev = step;
            step += step;
            if prev >= n:
                return -1;
        
        for i in range(prev, min(step, n)):
            if arr[i] == target:
                return i;
        return -1;

// Jump Search Analysis
define jump_search_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = list(range(1000000));
    target = random.randint(0, 1000000);
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, target);
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Jump Search Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(sqrt(n))
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Jump search analysis complete";
9.1.4 Interpolation Search

earth
// Interpolation Search
define interpolation_search:
    define search(arr, target):
        left = 0;
        right = len(arr) - 1;
        
        while left <= right and target >= arr[left] and target <= arr[right]:
            if left == right:
                if arr[left] == target:
                    return left;
                return -1;
            
            # Calculate interpolation position
            pos = left + int(((right - left) / (arr[right] - arr[left])) * (target - arr[left]));
            
            if arr[pos] == target:
                return pos;
            elif arr[pos] < target:
                left = pos + 1;
            else:
                right = pos - 1;
        return -1;
    
    // Interpolation search with safety checks
    define search_safe(arr, target):
        left = 0;
        right = len(arr) - 1;
        
        while left <= right and target >= arr[left] and target <= arr[right]:
            # Prevent division by zero
            if arr[right] == arr[left]:
                break;
            
            pos = left + int(((right - left) / (arr[right] - arr[left])) * (target - arr[left]));
            
            # Ensure pos is within bounds
            if pos < left or pos > right:
                break;
            
            if arr[pos] == target:
                return pos;
            elif arr[pos] < target:
                left = pos + 1;
            else:
                right = pos - 1;
        return -1;

// Interpolation Search Analysis
define interpolation_search_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate uniform data
    uniform_data = list(range(1000000));
    
    // Generate non-uniform data
    non_uniform_data = [i**2 for i in range(1000)];
    
    // Test uniform data
    start = time.time();
    search(uniform_data, 500000);
    uniform_time = time.time() - start;
    
    // Test non-uniform data
    start = time.time();
    search(non_uniform_data, 500000);
    non_uniform_time = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Interpolation Search Performance:");
    print(f"Uniform data: {uniform_time:.6f} seconds");
    print(f"Non-uniform data: {non_uniform_time:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(log log n) average, O(n) worst case
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Interpolation search analysis complete";
9.1.5 Exponential Search

earth
// Exponential Search
define exponential_search:
    // Basic exponential search
    define search(arr, target):
        n = len(arr);
        if arr[0] == target:
            return 0;
        
        # Find range for binary search
        i = 1;
        while i < n and arr[i] <= target:
            i *= 2;
        
        # Binary search in range
        left = i // 2;
        right = min(i, n - 1);
        
        while left <= right:
            mid = (left + right) // 2;
            if arr[mid] == target:
                return mid;
            elif arr[mid] < target:
                left = mid + 1;
            else:
                right = mid - 1;
        return -1;
    
    // Exponential search with custom start
    define search_from(arr, target, start=0):
        n = len(arr);
        if start >= n:
            return -1;
        if arr[start] == target:
            return start;
        
        i = start;
        while i < n and arr[i] <= target:
            i = max(i * 2, i + 1);
        
        left = start;
        right = min(i, n - 1);
        
        while left <= right:
            mid = (left + right) // 2;
            if arr[mid] == target:
                return mid;
            elif arr[mid] < target:
                left = mid + 1;
            else:
                right = mid - 1;
        return -1;

// Exponential Search Analysis
define exponential_search_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = list(range(1000000));
    target = random.randint(0, 1000000);
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    search(arr, target);
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Exponential Search Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(log n)
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Exponential search analysis complete";
9.2 Sorting Algorithms

9.2.1 Bubble Sort

earth
// Bubble Sort
define bubble_sort:
    // Basic bubble sort
    define sort(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        for i in range(n - 1):
            for j in range(n - i - 1):
                if arr[j] > arr[j + 1]:
                    arr[j], arr[j + 1] = arr[j + 1], arr[j];
        return arr;
    
    // Optimized bubble sort with early exit
    define sort_optimized(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        for i in range(n - 1):
            swapped = False;
            for j in range(n - i - 1):
                if arr[j] > arr[j + 1]:
                    arr[j], arr[j + 1] = arr[j + 1], arr[j];
                    swapped = True;
            if not swapped:
                break;
        return arr;
    
    // Bubble sort with custom comparison
    define sort_custom(arr, compare):
        n = len(arr);
        for i in range(n - 1):
            swapped = False;
            for j in range(n - i - 1):
                if compare(arr[j], arr[j + 1]) > 0:
                    arr[j], arr[j + 1] = arr[j + 1], arr[j];
                    swapped = True;
            if not swapped:
                break;
        return arr;
    
    // Cocktail sort (bidirectional bubble sort)
    define cocktail_sort(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        start = 0;
        end = n - 1;
        swapped = True;
        
        while swapped:
            swapped = False;
            
            # Forward pass
            for i in range(start, end):
                if arr[i] > arr[i + 1]:
                    arr[i], arr[i + 1] = arr[i + 1], arr[i];
                    swapped = True;
            
            if not swapped:
                break;
            
            swapped = False;
            end -= 1;
            
            # Backward pass
            for i in range(end - 1, start - 1, -1):
                if arr[i] > arr[i + 1]:
                    arr[i], arr[i + 1] = arr[i + 1], arr[i];
                    swapped = True;
            
            start += 1;
        
        return arr;

// Bubble Sort Analysis
define bubble_sort_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = [random.randint(0, 1000) for _ in range(1000)];
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    sort_optimized(arr.copy());
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Bubble Sort Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(n^2) worst case, O(n) best case
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Bubble sort analysis complete";
9.2.2 Selection Sort

earth
// Selection Sort
define selection_sort:
    // Basic selection sort
    define sort(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        for i in range(n):
            min_idx = i;
            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                if arr[j] < arr[min_idx]:
                    min_idx = j;
            arr[i], arr[min_idx] = arr[min_idx], arr[i];
        return arr;
    
    // Selection sort with custom comparison
    define sort_custom(arr, compare):
        n = len(arr);
        for i in range(n):
            min_idx = i;
            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                if compare(arr[j], arr[min_idx]) < 0:
                    min_idx = j;
            arr[i], arr[min_idx] = arr[min_idx], arr[i];
        return arr;
    
    // Selection sort for stability
    define sort_stable(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        for i in range(n):
            min_idx = i;
            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                if arr[j] < arr[min_idx]:
                    min_idx = j;
            # Shift elements to maintain stability
            if min_idx != i:
                value = arr[min_idx];
                for k in range(min_idx, i, -1):
                    arr[k] = arr[k - 1];
                arr[i] = value;
        return arr;

// Selection Sort Analysis
define selection_sort_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = [random.randint(0, 1000) for _ in range(1000)];
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    sort(arr.copy());
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Selection Sort Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(n^2) worst case, O(n^2) best case
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Selection sort analysis complete";
9.2.3 Insertion Sort

earth
// Insertion Sort
define insertion_sort:
    // Basic insertion sort
    define sort(arr):
        for i in range(1, len(arr)):
            key = arr[i];
            j = i - 1;
            while j >= 0 and arr[j] > key:
                arr[j + 1] = arr[j];
                j -= 1;
            arr[j + 1] = key;
        return arr;
    
    // Insertion sort with custom comparison
    define sort_custom(arr, compare):
        for i in range(1, len(arr)):
            key = arr[i];
            j = i - 1;
            while j >= 0 and compare(arr[j], key) > 0:
                arr[j + 1] = arr[j];
                j -= 1;
            arr[j + 1] = key;
        return arr;
    
    // Insertion sort with binary search
    define sort_binary(arr):
        import bisect;
        for i in range(1, len(arr)):
            key = arr[i];
            # Find insertion position using binary search
            pos = bisect.bisect_left(arr, key, 0, i);
            # Shift elements
            for j in range(i, pos, -1):
                arr[j] = arr[j - 1];
            arr[pos] = key;
        return arr;
    
    // Shell sort (improved insertion sort)
    define shell_sort(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        gap = n // 2;
        
        while gap > 0:
            for i in range(gap, n):
                temp = arr[i];
                j = i;
                while j >= gap and arr[j - gap] > temp:
                    arr[j] = arr[j - gap];
                    j -= gap;
                arr[j] = temp;
            gap //= 2;
        return arr;

// Insertion Sort Analysis
define insertion_sort_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = [random.randint(0, 1000) for _ in range(1000)];
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    sort(arr.copy());
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Insertion Sort Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(n^2) worst case, O(n) best case
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Insertion sort analysis complete";
9.2.4 Merge Sort

earth
// Merge Sort
define merge_sort:
    // Basic merge sort
    define sort(arr):
        if len(arr) <= 1:
            return arr;
        
        mid = len(arr) // 2;
        left = sort(arr[:mid]);
        right = sort(arr[mid:]);
        return merge(left, right);
    
    // Merge function
    define merge(left, right):
        result = [];
        i = j = 0;
        
        while i < len(left) and j < len(right):
            if left[i] <= right[j]:
                result.append(left[i]);
                i += 1;
            else:
                result.append(right[j]);
                j += 1;
        
        result.extend(left[i:]);
        result.extend(right[j:]);
        return result;
    
    // Merge sort with custom comparison
    define sort_custom(arr, compare):
        if len(arr) <= 1:
            return arr;
        
        mid = len(arr) // 2;
        left = sort_custom(arr[:mid], compare);
        right = sort_custom(arr[mid:], compare);
        return merge_custom(left, right, compare);
    
    define merge_custom(left, right, compare):
        result = [];
        i = j = 0;
        
        while i < len(left) and j < len(right):
            if compare(left[i], right[j]) <= 0:
                result.append(left[i]);
                i += 1;
            else:
                result.append(right[j]);
                j += 1;
        
        result.extend(left[i:]);
        result.extend(right[j:]);
        return result;
    
    // In-place merge sort (memory efficient)
    define sort_inplace(arr):
        if len(arr) <= 1:
            return arr;
        
        def merge_inplace(arr, left, mid, right):
            # Use temporary array
            temp = arr[left:right + 1];
            i = 0;
            j = mid - left + 1;
            k = left;
            len_left = mid - left + 1;
            
            while i < len_left and j < len(temp):
                if temp[i] <= temp[j]:
                    arr[k] = temp[i];
                    i += 1;
                else:
                    arr[k] = temp[j];
                    j += 1;
                k += 1;
            
            while i < len_left:
                arr[k] = temp[i];
                i += 1;
                k += 1;
        
        def sort_recursive(arr, left, right):
            if left < right:
                mid = (left + right) // 2;
                sort_recursive(arr, left, mid);
                sort_recursive(arr, mid + 1, right);
                merge_inplace(arr, left, mid, right);
        
        sort_recursive(arr, 0, len(arr) - 1);
        return arr;

// Merge Sort Analysis
define merge_sort_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = [random.randint(0, 1000) for _ in range(1000)];
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    sort(arr);
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Merge Sort Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(n log n) worst case
    // Space complexity: O(n)
    
    return "Merge sort analysis complete";
9.2.5 Quick Sort

earth
// Quick Sort
define quick_sort:
    // Basic quick sort
    define sort(arr):
        if len(arr) <= 1:
            return arr;
        
        pivot = arr[len(arr) // 2];
        left = [x for x in arr if x < pivot];
        middle = [x for x in arr if x == pivot];
        right = [x for x in arr if x > pivot];
        
        return sort(left) + middle + sort(right);
    
    // Quick sort with in-place partitioning
    define sort_inplace(arr, low=0, high=null):
        if high is null:
            high = len(arr) - 1;
        
        if low < high:
            pi = partition(arr, low, high);
            sort_inplace(arr, low, pi - 1);
            sort_inplace(arr, pi + 1, high);
        
        return arr;
    
    define partition(arr, low, high):
        pivot = arr[high];
        i = low - 1;
        
        for j in range(low, high):
            if arr[j] <= pivot:
                i += 1;
                arr[i], arr[j] = arr[j], arr[i];
        
        arr[i + 1], arr[high] = arr[high], arr[i + 1];
        return i + 1;
    
    // Quick sort with random pivot
    define sort_random(arr):
        import random;
        
        def partition_random(arr, low, high):
            # Random pivot
            pivot_idx = random.randint(low, high);
            arr[pivot_idx], arr[high] = arr[high], arr[pivot_idx];
            return partition(arr, low, high);
        
        def sort_recursive(arr, low, high):
            if low < high:
                pi = partition_random(arr, low, high);
                sort_recursive(arr, low, pi - 1);
                sort_recursive(arr, pi + 1, high);
        
        sort_recursive(arr, 0, len(arr) - 1);
        return arr;
    
    // Quick sort with median-of-three
    define sort_median(arr):
        def median_of_three(arr, low, high):
            mid = (low + high) // 2;
            # Sort the three elements
            if arr[low] > arr[mid]:
                arr[low], arr[mid] = arr[mid], arr[low];
            if arr[mid] > arr[high]:
                arr[mid], arr[high] = arr[high], arr[mid];
            if arr[low] > arr[mid]:
                arr[low], arr[mid] = arr[mid], arr[low];
            return mid;  # Return median index
        
        def partition_median(arr, low, high):
            # Use median-of-three as pivot
            pivot_idx = median_of_three(arr, low, high);
            arr[pivot_idx], arr[high] = arr[high], arr[pivot_idx];
            return partition(arr, low, high);
        
        def sort_recursive(arr, low, high):
            if low < high:
                pi = partition_median(arr, low, high);
                sort_recursive(arr, low, pi - 1);
                sort_recursive(arr, pi + 1, high);
        
        sort_recursive(arr, 0, len(arr) - 1);
        return arr;

// Quick Sort Analysis
define quick_sort_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = [random.randint(0, 1000) for _ in range(1000)];
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    sort(arr);
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Quick Sort Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(n log n) average, O(n^2) worst case
    // Space complexity: O(log n) average, O(n) worst case
    
    return "Quick sort analysis complete";
9.2.6 Heap Sort

earth
// Heap Sort
define heap_sort:
    // Basic heap sort
    define sort(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        
        # Build max heap
        for i in range(n // 2 - 1, -1, -1):
            heapify(arr, n, i);
        
        # Extract elements
        for i in range(n - 1, 0, -1):
            arr[i], arr[0] = arr[0], arr[i];
            heapify(arr, i, 0);
        
        return arr;
    
    // Heapify function
    define heapify(arr, n, i):
        largest = i;
        left = 2 * i + 1;
        right = 2 * i + 2;
        
        if left < n and arr[left] > arr[largest]:
            largest = left;
        if right < n and arr[right] > arr[largest]:
            largest = right;
        
        if largest != i:
            arr[i], arr[largest] = arr[largest], arr[i];
            heapify(arr, n, largest);
    
    // Heap sort with min heap (descending order)
    define sort_descending(arr):
        n = len(arr);
        
        # Build min heap
        for i in range(n // 2 - 1, -1, -1):
            heapify_min(arr, n, i);
        
        # Extract elements
        for i in range(n - 1, 0, -1):
            arr[i], arr[0] = arr[0], arr[i];
            heapify_min(arr, i, 0);
        
        return arr;
    
    // Min heapify
    define heapify_min(arr, n, i):
        smallest = i;
        left = 2 * i + 1;
        right = 2 * i + 2;
        
        if left < n and arr[left] < arr[smallest]:
            smallest = left;
        if right < n and arr[right] < arr[smallest]:
            smallest = right;
        
        if smallest != i:
            arr[i], arr[smallest] = arr[smallest], arr[i];
            heapify_min(arr, n, smallest);
    
    // Heap sort with custom comparison
    define sort_custom(arr, compare):
        n = len(arr);
        
        def heapify_custom(arr, n, i):
            extreme = i;
            left = 2 * i + 1;
            right = 2 * i + 2;
            
            if left < n and compare(arr[left], arr[extreme]) > 0:
                extreme = left;
            if right < n and compare(arr[right], arr[extreme]) > 0:
                extreme = right;
            
            if extreme != i:
                arr[i], arr[extreme] = arr[extreme], arr[i];
                heapify_custom(arr, n, extreme);
        
        for i in range(n // 2 - 1, -1, -1):
            heapify_custom(arr, n, i);
        
        for i in range(n - 1, 0, -1):
            arr[i], arr[0] = arr[0], arr[i];
            heapify_custom(arr, i, 0);
        
        return arr;

// Heap Sort Analysis
define heap_sort_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = [random.randint(0, 1000) for _ in range(1000)];
    
    // Performance test
    start = time.time();
    sort(arr);
    time_taken = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Heap Sort Performance:");
    print(f"Time taken: {time_taken:.6f} seconds");
    
    // Time complexity: O(n log n) worst case
    // Space complexity: O(1)
    
    return "Heap sort analysis complete";
9.2.7 Other Sorting Algorithms

earth
// Other Sorting Algorithms
define other_sorting:
    // Counting Sort
    define counting_sort(arr):
        if not arr:
            return arr;
        
        max_val = max(arr);
        min_val = min(arr);
        range_size = max_val - min_val + 1;
        
        # Count occurrences
        count = [0] * range_size;
        for num in arr:
            count[num - min_val] += 1;
        
        # Cumulative count
        for i in range(1, len(count)):
            count[i] += count[i - 1];
        
        # Build sorted array
        result = [0] * len(arr);
        for num in reversed(arr):
            count[num - min_val] -= 1;
            result[count[num - min_val]] = num;
        
        return result;
    
    // Radix Sort
    define radix_sort(arr):
        if not arr:
            return arr;
        
        max_val = max(arr);
        exp = 1;
        
        while max_val // exp > 0:
            counting_sort_by_digit(arr, exp);
            exp *= 10;
        
        return arr;
    
    define counting_sort_by_digit(arr, exp):
        n = len(arr);
        output = [0] * n;
        count = [0] * 10;
        
        # Count occurrences of each digit
        for i in range(n):
            digit = (arr[i] // exp) % 10;
            count[digit] += 1;
        
        # Cumulative count
        for i in range(1, 10):
            count[i] += count[i - 1];
        
        # Build output
        for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
            digit = (arr[i] // exp) % 10;
            count[digit] -= 1;
            output[count[digit]] = arr[i];
        
        # Copy back to original array
        for i in range(n):
            arr[i] = output[i];
    
    // Bucket Sort
    define bucket_sort(arr, bucket_size=5):
        if not arr:
            return arr;
        
        min_val = min(arr);
        max_val = max(arr);
        range_val = max_val - min_val + 1;
        num_buckets = (range_val + bucket_size - 1) // bucket_size;
        
        # Create empty buckets
        buckets = [[] for _ in range(num_buckets)];
        
        # Distribute elements into buckets
        for num in arr:
            index = (num - min_val) // bucket_size;
            buckets[index].append(num);
        
        # Sort each bucket and combine
        result = [];
        for bucket in buckets:
            if bucket:
                # Use insertion sort for small buckets
                insertion_sort(bucket);
                result.extend(bucket);
        
        return result;
    
    // Tim Sort (hybrid sorting)
    define tim_sort(arr):
        # Use Python's built-in sort (which is Timsort)
        return sorted(arr);

// Other Sorting Analysis
define other_sorting_analysis():
    import time;
    import random;
    
    // Generate test data
    arr = [random.randint(0, 1000) for _ in range(1000)];
    
    // Test Counting Sort
    start = time.time();
    counting_sort(arr);
    counting_time = time.time() - start;
    
    // Test Radix Sort
    start = time.time();
    radix_sort(arr);
    radix_time = time.time() - start;
    
    // Test Bucket Sort
    start = time.time();
    bucket_sort(arr);
    bucket_time = time.time() - start;
    
    print("Other Sorting Performance:");
    print(f"Counting Sort: {counting_time:.6f} seconds");
    print(f"Radix Sort: {radix_time:.6f} seconds");
    print(f"Bucket Sort: {bucket_time:.6f} seconds");
    
    return "Other sorting analysis complete";
9.3 Graph Algorithms

9.3.1 BFS (Breadth-First Search)

earth
// BFS Implementation
define graph_bfs:
    // BFS for adjacency list
    define bfs(graph, start):
        visited = [];
        queue = [start];
        visited.append(start);
        
        while queue:
            vertex = queue.pop(0);
            for neighbor in graph.get(vertex, []):
                if neighbor not in visited:
                    visited.append(neighbor);
                    queue.append(neighbor);
        
        return visited;
    
    // BFS with path tracking
    define bfs_path(graph, start, goal):
        visited = [];
        queue = [[start]];
        visited.append(start);
        
        while queue:
            path = queue.pop(0);
            vertex = path[-1];
            
            if vertex == goal:
                return path;
            
            for neighbor in graph.get(vertex, []):
                if neighbor not in visited:
                    visited.append(neighbor);
                    new_path = path + [neighbor];
                    queue.append(new_path);
        
        return null;
    
    // BFS for weighted graph
    define bfs_weighted(graph, start, goal):
        # Use queue with (vertex, cost, path)
        visited = {};
        queue = [(start, 0, [start])];
        visited[start] = 0;
        
        while queue:
            vertex, cost, path = queue.pop(0);
            
            if vertex == goal:
                return path, cost;
            
            for neighbor, weight in graph.get(vertex, []):
                new_cost = cost + weight;
                if neighbor not in visited or new_cost < visited[neighbor]:
                    visited[neighbor] = new_cost;
                    new_path = path + [neighbor];
                    queue.append((neighbor, new_cost, new_path));
        
        return null, float('inf');
    
    // BFS for grid (2D array)
    define bfs_grid(grid, start, goal):
        rows = len(grid);
        cols = len(grid[0]);
        
        # Directions: up, down, left, right
        directions = [(-1, 0), (1, 0), (0, -1), (0, 1)];
        queue = [(start[0], start[1], 0, [start])];
        visited = {start};
        
        while queue:
            r, c, dist, path = queue.pop(0);
            
            if (r, c) == goal:
                return path, dist;
            
            for dr, dc in directions:
                nr, nc = r + dr, c + dc;
                if 0 <= nr < rows and 0 <= nc < cols:
                    if grid[nr][nc] != '#' and (nr, nc) not in visited:
                        visited.add((nr, nc));
                        queue.append((nr, nc, dist + 1, path + [(nr, nc)]));
        
        return null, float('inf');

// BFS Applications
define bfs_applications():
    // 1. Shortest path in unweighted graph
    graph = {
        'A': ['B', 'C'],
        'B': ['D', 'E'],
        'C': ['F'],
        'D': [],
        'E': [],
        'F': []
    };
    
    path, dist = bfs_path(graph, 'A', 'F');
    print(f"Shortest path from A to F: {path}, distance: {len(path) - 1}");
    
    // 2. Connected components
    define connected_components(graph):
        visited = {};
        components = [];
        
        for vertex in graph:
            if vertex not in visited:
                component = bfs(graph, vertex);
                for v in component:
                    visited[v] = True;
                components.append(component);
        
        return components;
    
    graph2 = {
        'A': ['B'],
        'B': ['A'],
        'C': ['D'],
        'D': ['C'],
        'E': []
    };
    print(f"Connected components: {connected_components(graph2)}");
    
    // 3. Level-order traversal of tree
    class TreeNode:
        __init__(self, val):
            self.val = val;
            self.left = null;
            self.right = null;
    
    define level_order_traversal(root):
        if root is null:
            return [];
        
        result = [];
        queue = [root];
        
        while queue:
            level_size = len(queue);
            level = [];
            for _ in range(level_size):
                node = queue.pop(0);
                level.append(node.val);
                if node.left:
                    queue.append(node.left);
                if node.right:
                    queue.append(node.right);
            result.append(level);
        
        return result;
    
    return "BFS applications complete";
9.3.2 DFS (Depth-First Search)

earth
// DFS Implementation
define graph_dfs:
    // DFS using stack (iterative)
    define dfs_iterative(graph, start):
        visited = [];
        stack = [start];
        
        while stack:
            vertex = stack.pop();
            if vertex not in visited:
                visited.append(vertex);
                # Add neighbors in reverse order for natural traversal
                for neighbor in reversed(graph.get(vertex, [])):
                    if neighbor not in visited:
                        stack.append(neighbor);
        
        return visited;
    
    // DFS using recursion
    define dfs_recursive(graph, start, visited=null):
        if visited is null:
            visited = [];
        
        visited.append(start);
        for neighbor in graph.get(start, []):
            if neighbor not in visited:
                dfs_recursive(graph, neighbor, visited);
        
        return visited;
    
    // DFS with path tracking
    define dfs_path(graph, start, goal, path=null, visited=null):
        if path is null:
            path = [start];
        if visited is null:
            visited = {start};
        
        if start == goal:
            return path;
        
        for neighbor in graph.get(start, []):
            if neighbor not in visited:
                visited.add(neighbor);
                result = dfs_path(graph, neighbor, goal, path + [neighbor], visited);
                if result:
                    return result;
        
        return null;
    
    // DFS for weighted graph
    define dfs_weighted(graph, start, goal):
        # Use stack with (vertex, cost, path)
        stack = [(start, 0, [start])];
        visited = {start};
        best = {"path": null, "cost": float('inf')};
        
        while stack:
            vertex, cost, path = stack.pop();
            
            if vertex == goal and cost < best["cost"]:
                best["path"] = path;
                best["cost"] = cost;
                continue;
            
            for neighbor, weight in graph.get(vertex, []):
                if neighbor not in visited:
                    visited.add(neighbor);
                    stack.append((neighbor, cost + weight, path + [neighbor]));
        
        return best["path"], best["cost"];

// DFS Applications
define dfs_applications():
    // 1. Detecting cycles in a graph
    define has_cycle(graph):
        visited = {};
        rec_stack = {};
        
        define dfs_cycle(vertex):
            visited[vertex] = True;
            rec_stack[vertex] = True;
            
            for neighbor in graph.get(vertex, []):
                if neighbor not in visited:
                    if dfs_cycle(neighbor):
                        return True;
                elif rec_stack.get(neighbor, False):
                    return True;
            
            rec_stack[vertex] = False;
            return False;
        
        for vertex in graph:
            if vertex not in visited:
                if dfs_cycle(vertex):
                    return True;
        return False;
    
    graph_cycle = {
        'A': ['B'],
        'B': ['C'],
        'C': ['A']
    };
    print(f"Has cycle: {has_cycle(graph_cycle)}");
    
    // 2. Topological sorting
    define topological_sort(graph):
        visited = {};
        result = [];
        
        define dfs_sort(vertex):
            visited[vertex] = True;
            for neighbor in graph.get(vertex, []):
                if neighbor not in visited:
                    dfs_sort(neighbor);
            result.insert(0, vertex);  # Add to front
        
        for vertex in graph:
            if vertex not in visited:
                dfs_sort(vertex);
        
        return result;
    
    dag = {
        'A': ['C', 'D'],
        'B': ['D'],
        'C': ['E'],
        'D': ['E'],
        'E': []
    };
    print(f"Topological sort: {topological_sort(dag)}");
    
    // 3. Finding connected components
    define find_components(graph):
        visited = {};
        components = [];
        
        define dfs_component(vertex, component):
            visited[vertex] = True;
            component.append(vertex);
            for neighbor in graph.get(vertex, []):
                if neighbor not in visited:
                    dfs_component(neighbor, component);
        
        for vertex in graph:
            if vertex not in visited:
                component = [];
                dfs_component(vertex, component);
                components.append(component);
        
        return components;
    
    return "DFS applications complete";
}

9.4 Dynamic Programming

9.4.1 Fibonacci with DP

```earth
// Fibonacci with Dynamic Programming
define fibonacci_dp:
    // 1. Naive recursion (exponential)
    define fib_naive(n):
        if n <= 1:
            return n;
        return fib_naive(n - 1) + fib_naive(n - 2);
    
    // 2. Memoization (top-down DP)
    define fib_memo(n, memo=null):
        if memo is null:
            memo = {};
        
        if n <= 1:
            return n;
        if n in memo:
            return memo[n];
        
        memo[n] = fib_memo(n - 1, memo) + fib_memo(n - 2, memo);
        return memo[n];
    
    // 3. Tabulation (bottom-up DP)
    define fib_tab(n):
        if n <= 1:
            return n;
        
        dp = [0] * (n + 1);
        dp[1] = 1;
        
        for i in range(2, n + 1):
            dp[i] = dp[i - 1] + dp[i - 2];
        
        return dp[n];
    
    // 4. Space-optimized DP
    define fib_optimized(n):
        if n <= 1:
            return n;
        
        prev2 = 0;
        prev1 = 1;
        
        for i in range(2, n + 1):
            curr = prev1 + prev2;
            prev2 = prev1;
            prev1 = curr;
        
        return prev1;

// DP Performance Comparison
define fib_performance():
    import time;
    import sys;
    
    n = 30;
    
    # Test naive recursion (only for small n)
    if n < 35:
        start = time.time();
        result = fib_naive(n);
        time_naive = time.time() - start;
        print(f"Naive recursion: {time_naive:.6f}s, result: {result}");
    else:
        print("Naive recursion skipped (too slow)");
    
    # Test memoization
    start = time.time();
    result = fib_memo(n);
    time_memo = time.time() - start;
    print(f"Memoization: {time_memo:.6f}s, result: {result}");
    
    # Test tabulation
    start = time.time();
    result = fib_tab(n);
    time_tab = time.time() - start;
    print(f"Tabulation: {time_tab:.6f}s, result: {result}");
    
    # Test optimized DP
    start = time.time();
    result = fib_optimized(n);
    time_opt = time.time() - start;
    print(f"Optimized DP: {time_opt:.6f}s, result: {result}");
    
    return "Fibonacci DP comparison complete";
9.4.2 Longest Common Subsequence

earth
// Longest Common Subsequence (LCS)
define lcs:
    // LCS with DP table
    define lcs_length(str1, str2):
        m = len(str1);
        n = len(str2);
        
        # Create DP table
        dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)];
        
        # Fill DP table
        for i in range(1, m + 1):
            for j in range(1, n + 1):
                if str1[i - 1] == str2[j - 1]:
                    dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1;
                else:
                    dp[i][j] = max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]);
        
        return dp[m][n];
    
    // LCS with sequence retrieval
    define lcs_sequence(str1, str2):
        m = len(str1);
        n = len(str2);
        
        # Create DP table
        dp = [[0] * (n + 1) for _ in range(m + 1)];
        
        # Fill DP table
        for i in range(1, m + 1):
            for j in range(1, n + 1):
                if str1[i - 1] == str2[j - 1]:
                    dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1;
                else:
                    dp[i][j] = max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]);
        
        # Reconstruct LCS
        result = [];
        i = m;
        j = n;
        while i > 0 and j > 0:
            if str1[i - 1] == str2[j - 1]:
                result.insert(0, str1[i - 1]);
                i -= 1;
                j -= 1;
            elif dp[i - 1][j] > dp[i][j - 1]:
                i -= 1;
            else:
                j -= 1;
        
        return dp[m][n], ''.join(result);
    
    // LCS with space optimization
    define lcs_optimized(str1, str2):
        m = len(str1);
        n = len(str2);
        
        # Use two rows instead of full table
        prev = [0] * (n + 1);
        curr = [0] * (n + 1);
        
        for i in range(1, m + 1):
            for j in range(1, n + 1):
                if str1[i - 1] == str2[j - 1]:
                    curr[j] = prev[j - 1] + 1;
                else:
                    curr[j] = max(prev[j], curr[j - 1]);
            prev, curr = curr, prev;
        
        return prev[n];

// LCS Applications
define lcs_applications():
    // 1. File comparison (diff)
    define file_diff(file1, file2):
        # Split into lines
        lines1 = file1.splitlines();
        lines2 = file2.splitlines();
        
        # Find LCS of lines
        length, lcs = lcs_sequence(lines1, lines2);
        
        # Show differences
        print(f"Lines in common: {length}");
        print(f"Common lines: {lcs}");
        
        return "Diff complete";
    
    // 2. DNA sequence alignment
    define dna_alignment(seq1, seq2):
        # Compare DNA sequences
        length, lcs = lcs_sequence(seq1, seq2);
        similarity = (2 * length) / (len(seq1) + len(seq2));
        
        return {
            "length": length,
            "similarity": similarity,
            "aligned": lcs
        };
    
    seq1 = "ATCGATCG";
    seq2 = "ATCGGATC";
    alignment = dna_alignment(seq1, seq2);
    print(f"DNA alignment: {alignment}");
    
    return "LCS applications complete";

10.1 Classes and Objects

10.1.1 Class Definition

earth
// Class Definition Basics
define class_basics:
    // Simple class definition
    class Person:
        // Constructor
        __init__(self, name, age):
            self.name = name;
            self.age = age;
        
        // Instance method
        say_hello(self):
            print(f"Hello, I'm {self.name}");
        
        // Method with return
        get_info(self):
            return f"Person: {self.name}, Age: {self.age}";
        
        // Method with parameters
        have_birthday(self):
            self.age += 1;
            return f"{self.name} is now {self.age} years old";
    
    // Using the class
    person = Person("Alice", 25);
    person.say_hello();
    print(person.get_info());
    print(person.have_birthday());
    
    return "Class basics complete";

// Class with properties
define class_with_properties:
    class Person:
        __init__(self, name, age):
            self._name = name;
            self._age = age;
        
        // Getter
        @property
        def name(self):
            return self._name;
        
        // Setter
        @name.setter
        def name(self, value):
            self._name = value;
        
        @property
        def age(self):
            return self._age;
        
        @age.setter
        def age(self, value):
            if value < 0:
                raise ValueError("Age cannot be negative");
            self._age = value;
        
        // Deleter
        @age.deleter
        def age(self):
            del self._age;
    
    // Using properties
    person = Person("Alice", 25);
    print(person.name);  # Alice
    person.name = "Bob";
    print(person.name);  # Bob
    print(person.age);   # 25
    
    // person.age = -5;  # ValueError
    
    return "Class properties complete";
10.1.2 Class and Instance Methods

earth
// Class Methods and Static Methods
define class_methods:
    class Person:
        // Class variable
        species = "Homo sapiens";
        population = 0;
        
        __init__(self, name, age):
            self.name = name;
            self.age = age;
            Person.population += 1;
        
        // Instance method
        say_hello(self):
            return f"Hello, I'm {self.name}";
        
        // Class method
        @classmethod
        def get_species(cls):
            return cls.species;
        
        @classmethod
        def create_baby(cls, name):
            return cls(name, 0);
        
        // Static method
        @staticmethod
        def is_adult(age):
            return age >= 18;
        
        @staticmethod
        def validate_name(name):
            return len(name) >= 2;
        
        // Destructor
        __del__(self):
            Person.population -= 1;
    
    // Using class methods
    print(Person.get_species());  # Homo sapiens
    
    // Creating via class method
    baby = Person.create_baby("Charlie");
    print(baby.name, baby.age);  # Charlie 0
    
    // Static methods
    print(Person.is_adult(25));  # True
    print(Person.validate_name("A"));  # False
    
    // Class variable
    print(Person.population);  # 2
    
    return "Class methods complete";
10.1.3 Instance Attributes

earth
// Instance Attributes
define instance_attributes:
    class Student:
        __init__(self, name, grade):
            # Public attributes
            self.name = name;
            self.grade = grade;
            
            # Private attributes (by convention)
            self._id = self._generate_id();
            
            # Using setter for validation
            self._set_grade(grade);
        
        def _generate_id(self):
            import uuid;
            return str(uuid.uuid4())[:8];
        
        def _set_grade(self, grade):
            if grade < 0 or grade > 100:
                raise ValueError("Grade must be between 0 and 100");
            self._grade = grade;
        
        @property
        def grade(self):
            return self._grade;
        
        @grade.setter
        def grade(self, value):
            self._set_grade(value);
        
        @property
        def id(self):
            return self._id;
        
        # Dynamic attribute creation
        def set_extra(self, key, value):
            setattr(self, key, value);
        
        def get_extra(self, key, default=null):
            return getattr(self, key, default);
        
        def has_extra(self, key):
            return hasattr(self, key);
        
        def list_attributes(self):
            return [attr for attr in dir(self) if not attr.startswith("_")];
    
    // Using instance attributes
    student = Student("Alice", 85);
    print(f"Name: {student.name}, Grade: {student.grade}");
    print(f"ID: {student.id}");
    
    # Dynamic attribute
    student.set_extra("email", "alice@example.com");
    print(student.get_extra("email"));
    print(student.has_extra("email"));
    
    print(student.list_attributes());
    
    return "Instance attributes complete";
10.2 Inheritance

10.2.1 Single Inheritance

earth
// Single Inheritance
define single_inheritance:
    // Base class
    class Animal:
        __init__(self, name, age):
            self.name = name;
            self.age = age;
        
        def speak(self):
            return "Some sound";
        
        def get_info(self):
            return f"Animal: {self.name}, Age: {self.age}";
        
        def __str__(self):
            return f"Animal({self.name})";
    
    // Derived class
    class Dog(Animal):
        __init__(self, name, age, breed):
            # Call parent constructor
            super().__init__(name, age);
            self.breed = breed;
        
        // Method overriding
        def speak(self):
            return "Woof!";
        
        // Additional method
        def fetch(self, item):
            return f"{self.name} fetches the {item}";
        
        // Extending parent method
        def get_info(self):
            parent_info = super().get_info();
            return f"{parent_info}, Breed: {self.breed}";
    
    // Another derived class
    class Cat(Animal):
        __init__(self, name, age, color):
            super().__init__(name, age);
            self.color = color;
        
        def speak(self):
            return "Meow!";
        
        def climb(self):
            return f"{self.name} climbs a tree";
    
    // Using inheritance
    dog = Dog("Rex", 3, "German Shepherd");
    cat = Cat("Whiskers", 2, "Orange");
    
    print(dog.speak());  # Woof!
    print(cat.speak());  # Meow!
    print(dog.get_info());
    print(cat.get_info());
    print(dog.fetch("ball"));
    print(cat.climb());
    
    // Type checking
    print(isinstance(dog, Dog));  # True
    print(isinstance(dog, Animal));  # True
    print(isinstance(dog, Cat));  # False
    
    return "Single inheritance complete";
10.2.2 Multiple Inheritance

earth
// Multiple Inheritance
define multiple_inheritance:
    // Parent classes
    class Flyable:
        def fly(self):
            return "Flying";
        
        def get_wing_count(self):
            return 2;
    
    class Swimmable:
        def swim(self):
            return "Swimming";
        
        def get_fin_count(self):
            return 2;
    
    class Walkable:
        def walk(self):
            return "Walking";
        
        def get_leg_count(self):
            return 4;
    
    // Child class with multiple inheritance
    class Duck(Flyable, Swimmable, Walkable):
        __init__(self, name):
            self.name = name;
        
        def get_info(self):
            return f"Duck: {self.name}";
        
        # Override method
        def walk(self):
            return f"{self.name} waddles";
        
        # Method resolution order
        def get_movement_count(self):
            # Access parent methods
            wings = self.get_wing_count();
            fins = self.get_fin_count();
            legs = self.get_leg_count();
            return {
                "wings": wings,
                "fins": fins,
                "legs": legs
            };
    
    // Another child class
    class FlyingFish(Flyable, Swimmable):
        __init__(self, name):
            self.name = name;
        
        def fly(self):
            return f"{self.name} glides through the air";
        
        def swim(self):
            return f"{self.name} swims gracefully";
    
    // Using multiple inheritance
    duck = Duck("Donald");
    print(duck.fly());  # Flying
    print(duck.swim());  # Swimming
    print(duck.walk());  # Donald waddles
    print(duck.get_movement_count());
    
    fish = FlyingFish("Magikarp");
    print(fish.fly());
    print(fish.swim());
    
    // Method Resolution Order (MRO)
    print(Duck.__mro__);
    
    return "Multiple inheritance complete";
10.2.3 Multilevel Inheritance

earth
// Multilevel Inheritance
define multilevel_inheritance:
    // Level 1
    class Vehicle:
        __init__(self, make, model):
            self.make = make;
            self.model = model;
        
        def start(self):
            return "Vehicle started";
        
        def get_info(self):
            return f"{self.make} {self.model}";
    
    // Level 2
    class Car(Vehicle):
        __init__(self, make, model, doors):
            super().__init__(make, model);
            self.doors = doors;
        
        def honk(self):
            return "Beep beep!";
        
        def start(self):
            return f"Car started with key";
    
    // Level 3
    class ElectricCar(Car):
        __init__(self, make, model, doors, battery_capacity):
            super().__init__(make, model, doors);
            self.battery_capacity = battery_capacity;
        
        def charge(self):
            return "Charging the battery";
        
        def start(self):
            return f"Electric car started silently";
        
        def get_info(self):
            base_info = super().get_info();
            return f"{base_info} (Electric, {self.battery_capacity} kWh)";
    
    // Level 4
    class Tesla(ElectricCar):
        __init__(self, model, doors, battery_capacity, autopilot_version):
            super().__init__("Tesla", model, doors, battery_capacity);
            self.autopilot_version = autopilot_version;
        
        def autopilot(self):
            return f"Autopilot version {self.autopilot_version} engaged";
        
        def start(self):
            return f"Tesla started with app";
    
    // Using multilevel inheritance
    tesla = Tesla("Model S", 4, 100, "3.0");
    print(tesla.get_info());
    print(tesla.start());
    print(tesla.charge());
    print(tesla.autopilot());
    print(tesla.honk());
    
    // Check inheritance chain
    print(isinstance(tesla, Tesla));  # True
    print(isinstance(tesla, ElectricCar));  # True
    print(isinstance(tesla, Car));  # True
    print(isinstance(tesla, Vehicle));  # True
    
    return "Multilevel inheritance complete";
10.2.4 Hierarchical Inheritance

earth
// Hierarchical Inheritance
define hierarchical_inheritance:
    // Base class
    class Shape:
        __init__(self, color="black"):
            self.color = color;
        
        def area(self):
            return 0;
        
        def perimeter(self):
            return 0;
        
        def get_color(self):
            return self.color;
        
        def __str__(self):
            return f"Shape(color={self.color})";
    
    // Multiple derived classes from same base
    class Circle(Shape):
        __init__(self, radius, color="black"):
            super().__init__(color);
            self.radius = radius;
        
        def area(self):
            return 3.14159 * self.radius ** 2;
        
        def perimeter(self):
            return 2 * 3.14159 * self.radius;
        
        def get_diameter(self):
            return 2 * self.radius;
        
        def __str__(self):
            return f"Circle(radius={self.radius}, color={self.color})";
    
    class Rectangle(Shape):
        __init__(self, width, height, color="black"):
            super().__init__(color);
            self.width = width;
            self.height = height;
        
        def area(self):
            return self.width * self.height;
        
        def perimeter(self):
            return 2 * (self.width + self.height);
        
        def get_diagonal(self):
            return (self.width ** 2 + self.height ** 2) ** 0.5;
        
        def __str__(self):
            return f"Rectangle({self.width}x{self.height}, color={self.color})";
    
    class Triangle(Shape):
        __init__(self, side1, side2, side3, color="black"):
            super().__init__(color);
            self.side1 = side1;
            self.side2 = side2;
            self.side3 = side3;
        
        def area(self):
            # Heron's formula
            s = (self.side1 + self.side2 + self.side3) / 2;
            return (s * (s - self.side1) * (s - self.side2) * (s - self.side3)) ** 0.5;
        
        def perimeter(self):
            return self.side1 + self.side2 + self.side3;
        
        def is_equilateral(self):
            return self.side1 == self.side2 == self.side3;
        
        def __str__(self):
            return f"Triangle({self.side1},{self.side2},{self.side3}, color={self.color})";
    
    // Using hierarchical inheritance
    shapes = [
        Circle(5, "red"),
        Rectangle(4, 6, "blue"),
        Triangle(3, 4, 5, "green")
    ];
    
    for shape in shapes:
        print(shape);
        print(f"  Area: {shape.area():.2f}");
        print(f"  Perimeter: {shape.perimeter():.2f}");
        print(f"  Color: {shape.get_color()}");
        print("-" * 30);
    
    // Polymorphism with hierarchy
    define print_area(shape):
        print(f"Area of {shape}: {shape.area():.2f}");
    
    for shape in shapes:
        print_area(shape);
    
    return "Hierarchical inheritance complete";
10.3 Polymorphism

10.3.1 Method Overloading

earth
// Method Overloading
define method_overloading:
    // Using default parameters
    class Calculator:
        // Single method with defaults
        def add(self, a, b=0, c=0):
            return a + b + c;
        
        // Method with variable arguments
        def multiply(self, *args):
            if not args:
                return 0;
            result = 1;
            for num in args:
                result *= num;
            return result;
        
        // Method with type checking
        def process(self, data, format_type="json"):
            if isinstance(data, int):
                return data * 2;
            elif isinstance(data, str):
                return data.upper();
            elif isinstance(data, list):
                return len(data);
            else:
                return str(data);
    
    // Using method overloading
    calc = Calculator();
    
    # Different ways to call add
    print(calc.add(10));       # 10
    print(calc.add(10, 20));   # 30
    print(calc.add(10, 20, 30)); # 60
    
    # Variable arguments
    print(calc.multiply(2, 3));     # 6
    print(calc.multiply(2, 3, 4));  # 24
    print(calc.multiply(2, 3, 4, 5)); # 120
    
    # Type-based processing
    print(calc.process(42));        # 84
    print(calc.process("hello"));   # HELLO
    print(calc.process([1, 2, 3])); # 3
    
    // Using dispatch for more flexibility
    class Dispatcher:
        def dispatch(self, *args, **kwargs):
            # Determine which method to call based on args
            if len(args) == 1 and isinstance(args[0], int):
                return self._process_int(args[0]);
            elif len(args) == 1 and isinstance(args[0], str):
                return self._process_str(args[0]);
            elif len(args) == 2 and isinstance(args[0], str) and isinstance(args[1], int):
                return self._process_str_int(args[0], args[1]);
            else:
                return self._process_unknown(args, kwargs);
        
        def _process_int(self, value):
            return f"Processing int: {value * 2}";
        
        def _process_str(self, value):
            return f"Processing string: {value.upper()}";
        
        def _process_str_int(self, str_val, int_val):
            return f"String: {str_val}, Int: {int_val * 2}";
        
        def _process_unknown(self, args, kwargs):
            return f"Unknown: args={args}, kwargs={kwargs}";
    
    disp = Dispatcher();
    print(disp.dispatch(42));
    print(disp.dispatch("hello"));
    print(disp.dispatch("hello", 42));
    print(disp.dispatch("hello", 42, extra="value"));
    
    return "Method overloading complete";
10.3.2 Method Overriding

earth
// Method Overriding
define method_overriding:
    // Base class
    class Animal:
        def __init__(self, name):
            self.name = name;
        
        def speak(self):
            return "Some sound";
        
        def move(self):
            return "Moves somehow";
        
        def eat(self, food):
            return f"Eats {food}";
        
        def get_info(self):
            return f"Animal: {self.name}";
    
    // Overriding methods
    class Bird(Animal):
        def __init__(self, name, wing_span):
            super().__init__(name);
            self.wing_span = wing_span;
        
        def speak(self):
            return "Chirp!";
        
        def move(self):
            return "Flies";
        
        def eat(self, food):
            if food.lower() in ["seeds", "worms", "insects"]:
                return f"Bird eats {food}";
            else:
                return f"Bird doesn't eat {food}";
        
        def get_info(self):
            return f"Bird: {self.name}, Wing span: {self.wing_span}";
    
    class Fish(Animal):
        def __init__(self, name, fin_count):
            super().__init__(name);
            self.fin_count = fin_count;
        
        def speak(self):
            return "... (silent)";
        
        def move(self):
            return "Swims";
        
        def eat(self, food):
            return f"Fish eats {food} (if it's food)";
        
        def get_info(self):
            return f"Fish: {self.name}, Fins: {self.fin_count}";
    
    // Using method overriding
    animals = [
        Animal("Generic Animal"),
        Bird("Tweety", 20),
        Fish("Nemo", 5)
    ];
    
    for animal in animals:
        print(f"{animal.get_info()}");
        print(f"  Speak: {animal.speak()}");
        print(f"  Move: {animal.move()}");
        print(f"  Eat: {animal.eat('worms')}");
        print("-" * 30);
    
    // Overriding with super()
    class AdvancedBird(Bird):
        def __init__(self, name, wing_span, color):
            super().__init__(name, wing_span);
            self.color = color;
        
        def speak(self):
            # Extend parent method
            base_sound = super().speak();
            return f"{base_sound} (beautiful song)";
        
        def move(self):
            return "Glides gracefully";
        
        def get_info(self):
            base_info = super().get_info();
            return f"{base_info}, Color: {self.color}";
    
    bird = AdvancedBird("Eagle", 80, "Brown");
    print(bird.get_info());
    print(f"  Speak: {bird.speak()}");
    print(f"  Move: {bird.move()}");
    
    return "Method overriding complete";
}

10.3.3 Operator Overloading

```earth
// Operator Overloading
define operator_overloading:
    class Vector:
        __init__(self, x=0, y=0):
            self.x = x;
            self.y = y;
        
        // String representation
        def __str__(self):
            return f"Vector({self.x}, {self.y})";
        
        def __repr__(self):
            return f"Vector({self.x}, {self.y})";
        
        // Addition
        def __add__(self, other):
            return Vector(self.x + other.x, self.y + other.y);
        
        def __radd__(self, other):
            return self.__add__(other);
        
        // Subtraction
        def __sub__(self, other):
            return Vector(self.x - other.x, self.y - other.y);
        
        // Multiplication (scalar or dot product)
        def __mul__(self, other):
            if isinstance(other, (int, float)):
                return Vector(self.x * other, self.y * other);
            elif isinstance(other, Vector):
                return self.x * other.x + self.y * other.y;
            else:
                raise TypeError("Unsupported multiplication");
        
        def __rmul__(self, other):
            return self.__mul__(other);
        
        // Division
        def __truediv__(self, scalar):
            if scalar == 0:
                raise ValueError("Cannot divide by zero");
            return Vector(self.x / scalar, self.y / scalar);
        
        // Equality
        def __eq__(self, other):
            if not isinstance(other, Vector):
                return False;
            return self.x == other.x and self.y == other.y;
        
        // Inequality
        def __ne__(self, other):
            return not self.__eq__(other);
        
        // Less than (magnitude comparison)
        def __lt__(self, other):
            return self.magnitude() < other.magnitude();
        
        def __le__(self, other):
            return self.magnitude() <= other.magnitude();
        
        def __gt__(self, other):
            return self.magnitude() > other.magnitude();
        
        def __ge__(self, other):
            return self.magnitude() >= other.magnitude();
        
        // Unary operators
        def __neg__(self):
            return Vector(-self.x, -self.y);
        
        def __pos__(self):
            return Vector(self.x, self.y);
        
        def __abs__(self):
            return self.magnitude();
        
        // Indexing
        def __getitem__(self, key):
            if key == 0:
                return self.x;
            elif key == 1:
                return self.y;
            else:
                raise IndexError("Index out of range");
        
        def __setitem__(self, key, value):
            if key == 0:
                self.x = value;
            elif key == 1:
                self.y = value;
            else:
                raise IndexError("Index out of range");
        
        // Length
        def __len__(self):
            return 2;
        
        // Iteration
        def __iter__(self):
            yield self.x;
            yield self.y;
        
        // Callable
        def __call__(self, scalar):
            return self.__mul__(scalar);
        
        // Additional methods
        def magnitude(self):
            return (self.x ** 2 + self.y ** 2) ** 0.5;
        
        def normalize(self):
            mag = self.magnitude();
            if mag == 0:
                return Vector(0, 0);
            return Vector(self.x / mag, self.y / mag);
    
    // Using operator overloading
    v1 = Vector(3, 4);
    v2 = Vector(1, 2);
    
    print(v1);  # Vector(3, 4)
    print(v1 + v2);  # Vector(4, 6)
    print(v1 - v2);  # Vector(2, 2)
    print(v1 * 2);  # Vector(6, 8)
    print(v1 * v2);  # 11 (dot product)
    print(v1 / 2);  # Vector(1.5, 2.0)
    print(v1 == Vector(3, 4));  # True
    print(v1 < v2);  # False (magnitude comparison)
    
    # Unary operators
    print(-v1);  # Vector(-3, -4)
    print(abs(v1));  # 5.0
    
    # Indexing
    print(v1[0]);  # 3
    v1[1] = 5;
    print(v1);  # Vector(3, 5)
    
    # Iteration
    for i in v1:
        print(i);  # 3, 5
    
    # Callable
    print(v1(2));  # Vector(6, 10)
    
    return "Operator overloading complete";
}

10.3.4 Duck Typing

```earth
// Duck Typing
define duck_typing:
    // Classes with similar interface
    class Duck:
        def quack(self):
            return "Quack!";
        
        def swim(self):
            return "Duck swimming";
        
        def fly(self):
            return "Duck flying";
    
    class Person:
        def quack(self):
            return "Person imitating duck";
        
        def swim(self):
            return "Person swimming";
        
        def walk(self):
            return "Person walking";
    
    class Robot:
        def quack(self):
            return "Robot duck sound";
        
        def beep(self):
            return "Beep beep!";
    
    // Function using duck typing
    def make_it_quack(obj):
        # Check if object has quack method
        if hasattr(obj, "quack"):
            return obj.quack();
        else:
            return f"{type(obj).__name__} cannot quack";
    
    def perform_duck_like(obj):
        # Use duck typing - if it walks like a duck and talks like a duck...
        result = [];
        if hasattr(obj, "quack"):
            result.append(obj.quack());
        if hasattr(obj, "swim"):
            result.append(obj.swim());
        if hasattr(obj, "fly"):
            result.append(obj.fly());
        return result or ["Not duck-like"];
    
    // Using duck typing
    duck = Duck();
    person = Person();
    robot = Robot();
    
    print(make_it_quack(duck));  # Quack!
    print(make_it_quack(person));  # Person imitating duck
    print(make_it_quack(robot));  # Robot duck sound
    
    print(perform_duck_like(duck));  # ['Quack!', 'Duck swimming', 'Duck flying']
    print(perform_duck_like(person));  # ['Person imitating duck', 'Person swimming']
    print(perform_duck_like(robot));  # ['Robot duck sound', 'Not duck-like']
    
    // Duck typing with function parameter
    def process_duck(obj):
        # Try to call common methods
        try:
            return obj.quack();
        except AttributeError:
            return f"{type(obj).__name__} doesn't support quack";
    
    // With different objects
    class Animal:
        def quack(self):
            return "Animal quacking";
    
    print(process_duck(Duck()));
    print(process_duck(Animal()));
    print(process_duck(Robot()));
    
    return "Duck typing complete";
}

10.4 Encapsulation

10.4.1 Public, Protected, Private Members

```earth
// Encapsulation
define encapsulation:
    class BankAccount:
        # Class variable (public)
        bank_name = "Global Bank";
        account_count = 0;
        
        __init__(self, owner, initial_balance=0):
            # Public attribute
            self.owner = owner;
            
            # Protected attribute (convention: single underscore)
            self._account_number = self._generate_account_number();
            
            # Private attribute (name mangling: double underscore)
            self.__balance = initial_balance;
            
            # Private class variable access
            BankAccount.account_count += 1;
        
        def _generate_account_number(self):
            # Protected method
            import random;
            return f"ACC-{random.randint(1000, 9999)}";
        
        # Public methods
        def deposit(self, amount):
            if amount <= 0:
                raise ValueError("Amount must be positive");
            self.__balance += amount;
            return self.__balance;
        
        def withdraw(self, amount):
            if amount <= 0:
                raise ValueError("Amount must be positive");
            if amount > self.__balance:
                raise ValueError("Insufficient balance");
            self.__balance -= amount;
            return self.__balance;
        
        def get_balance(self):
            # Getter for private attribute
            return self.__balance;
        
        def get_account_info(self):
            return {
                "owner": self.owner,
                "account_number": self._account_number,
                "balance": self.__balance,
                "bank": self.bank_name
            };
        
        # Private method
        def __validate_amount(self, amount):
            return amount > 0;
        
        # Property for controlled access
        @property
        def balance(self):
            return self.__balance;
        
        @balance.setter
        def balance(self, value):
            # Don't allow direct setting of balance
            raise AttributeError("Balance cannot be set directly");
        
        @balance.deleter
        def balance(self):
            raise AttributeError("Balance cannot be deleted");
        
        # Static method
        @staticmethod
        def get_bank_info():
            return f"Bank: {BankAccount.bank_name}, Total accounts: {BankAccount.account_count}";
    
    // Using encapsulation
    account = BankAccount("Alice", 1000);
    
    # Accessing public
    print(account.owner);  # Alice
    print(account.bank_name);  # Global Bank
    
    # Accessing protected (possible but discouraged)
    print(account._account_number);  # ACC-xxxx
    
    # Accessing private (name mangling)
    # print(account.__balance)  # AttributeError
    print(account._BankAccount__balance);  # 1000 (name mangling)
    
    # Using public methods
    account.deposit(500);
    print(account.get_balance());  # 1500
    
    # Using property
    print(account.balance);  # 1500
    
    # account.balance = 2000  # AttributeError
    
    # Static method
    print(BankAccount.get_bank_info());
    
    return "Encapsulation complete";
}

CHAPTER 11: EXCEPTION HANDLING
